`agent/mac/src/tailnet.rs`:

```rust
//! Which addresses count as "the tailnet", and the ICE candidate filter built on it.
//!
//! The range is written as numbers, not as text, so the public-repository hygiene scan
//! (which looks for address-shaped text) has nothing to flag in this file.
use std::net::IpAddr;

/// IPv4 100.64/10 (second octet 64..=127) and IPv6 fd7a:115c:a1e0::/48.
pub fn is_tailnet_ip(ip: IpAddr) -> bool {
    match ip {
        IpAddr::V4(v4) => {
            let o = v4.octets();
            o[0] == 100 && (64..=127).contains(&o[1])
        }
        IpAddr::V6(v6) => {
            if let Some(v4) = v6.to_ipv4_mapped() {
                return is_tailnet_ip(IpAddr::V4(v4));
            }
            let s = v6.segments();
            s[0] == 0xfd7a && s[1] == 0x115c && s[2] == 0xa1e0
        }
    }
}

/// Removes every `a=candidate:` line of an SDP whose address is not a literal tailnet
/// UDP address. mDNS names (`<uuid>.local`), LAN and public addresses, relay and TCP
/// candidates all go. Other lines, and the line ending style, are left exactly as they were.
pub fn filter_offer_candidates(sdp: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(sdp.len());
    for line in sdp.split_inclusive('\n') {
        let text = line.trim_end_matches(['\r', '\n']);
        if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix("a=candidate:") {
            if !candidate_allowed(rest) {
                continue;
            }
        }
        out.push_str(line);
    }
    out
}

// rest = "<foundation> <component> <transport> <priority> <address> <port> typ <type> ..."
fn candidate_allowed(rest: &str) -> bool {
    let mut parts = rest.split_whitespace();
    let (_foundation, _component, transport, _priority, address) =
        (parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next());
    match (transport, address.and_then(|a| a.parse::<IpAddr>().ok())) {
        (Some(t), Some(ip)) => t.eq_ignore_ascii_case("udp") && is_tailnet_ip(ip),
        _ => false,
    }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/config.rs`:

```rust
//! The agent's settings file and token file: parsing and the ownership rules that make
//! the agent refuse to start on a file somebody else could have rewritten (spec D2, D6).
//! File metadata is passed in as plain numbers so every rule is testable on any host.
use crate::tailnet::is_tailnet_ip;
use serde_json::Value;
use std::fmt;
use std::net::IpAddr;

pub const DEFAULT_PORT: u16 = 18471;

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ConfigError(pub String);
impl fmt::Display for ConfigError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}
impl std::error::Error for ConfigError {}
fn err<T>(message: impl Into<String>) -> Result<T, ConfigError> {
    Err(ConfigError(message.into()))
}

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Clone)]
pub struct AgentConfig {
    /// The Mac's own tailnet address: the HTTP server and the media socket bind here.
    pub bind: IpAddr,
    pub port: u16,
    /// The only address HTTP connections are accepted from.
    pub gateway: IpAddr,
}

/// `{"bind":"100.64.0.20","port":18471,"gateway":"100.64.0.10"}`; `port` is optional.
pub fn parse_config(text: &str) -> Result<AgentConfig, ConfigError> {
    let value: Value = serde_json::from_str(text).map_err(|_| ConfigError("agent.json is not valid JSON".into()))?;
    let Some(object) = value.as_object() else {
        return err("agent.json must be a JSON object");
    };
    for key in object.keys() {
        if !["bind", "port", "gateway"].contains(&key.as_str()) {
            return err(format!("agent.json has an unknown setting {key:?}"));
        }
    }
    let address = |name: &str| -> Result<IpAddr, ConfigError> {
        let Some(text) = object.get(name).and_then(Value::as_str) else {
            return err(format!("agent.json needs \"{name}\" (a tailnet address)"));
        };
        let Ok(ip) = text.parse::<IpAddr>() else {
            return err(format!("\"{name}\" is not an IP address"));
        };
        if !is_tailnet_ip(ip) {
            return err(format!("\"{name}\" must be a tailnet address"));
        }
        Ok(ip)
    };
    let bind = address("bind")?;
    let gateway = address("gateway")?;
    let port = match object.get("port") {
        None => DEFAULT_PORT,
        Some(p) => match p.as_u64() {
            Some(n) if (1024..=65535).contains(&n) => n as u16,
            _ => return err("\"port\" must be a number from 1024 to 65535"),
        },
    };
    Ok(AgentConfig { bind, port, gateway })
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Kind {
    File,
    Dir,
    /// A symbolic link, a socket, anything else. Links are never followed.
    Other,
}

/// Owner and permission bits of a path, as `lstat` reports them.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct FileMeta {
    pub mode: u32,
    pub uid: u32,
    pub kind: Kind,
}

/// The settings file must be a regular file owned by `owner_uid` (root in the operational
/// install) that nobody else can write: the owner is what decides who may control the
/// Mac. `user_uid` is the user running the agent, who must be able to read it.
pub fn check_config_file(meta: &FileMeta, owner_uid: u32, user_uid: u32) -> Result<(), ConfigError> {
    if meta.kind != Kind::File {
        return err("agent.json must be a regular file");
    }
    if meta.uid != owner_uid {
        return err(format!("agent.json must be owned by uid {owner_uid}"));
    }
    if meta.mode & 0o022 != 0 {
        return err("agent.json must not be writable by group or others");
    }
    if meta.uid != user_uid && meta.mode & 0o004 == 0 {
        return err("agent.json must be readable by the agent's user (mode 644)");
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// The directory that holds the settings file must belong to the same owner and be closed
/// to everybody else; otherwise the file could be replaced by renaming it.
pub fn check_config_dir(meta: &FileMeta, owner_uid: u32) -> Result<(), ConfigError> {
    if meta.kind != Kind::Dir {
        return err("the settings directory must be a real directory");
    }
    if meta.uid != owner_uid {
        return err(format!("the settings directory must be owned by uid {owner_uid}"));
    }
    if meta.mode & 0o022 != 0 {
        return err("the settings directory must not be writable by group or others");
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// The token file must be a regular file, mode exactly 600, owned by the user running the agent.
pub fn check_token_file(meta: &FileMeta, user_uid: u32) -> Result<(), ConfigError> {
    if meta.kind != Kind::File {
        return err("the token file must be a regular file");
    }
    if meta.mode & 0o777 != 0o600 {
        return err("the token file must have mode 600");
    }
    if meta.uid != user_uid {
        return err("the token file must be owned by the user running the agent");
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// 32 random bytes in base64url without padding: exactly 43 characters. A trailing
/// newline (from `cat` or an editor) is tolerated.
pub fn parse_token(text: &str) -> Result<String, ConfigError> {
    let token = text.trim_end_matches(['\n', '\r']);
    let valid = token.len() == 43 && token.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b == b'-' || b == b'_');
    if !valid {
        return err("the token file must hold 43 base64url characters");
    }
    Ok(token.to_string())
}
```

`agent/mac/src/auth.rs`:

```rust
//! Who may talk to the agent's HTTP server (spec D2): only the configured gateway
//! address, only with `Host` equal to the bound address and port, never with an `Origin`
//! header (a browser sends one; the gateway does not), and only with the bearer token.
use crate::tailnet::is_tailnet_ip;
use std::net::{IpAddr, SocketAddr};
use std::time::Duration;
use subtle::ConstantTimeEq;

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Reject {
    /// 403: the caller is not the gateway, or the request looks like a browser's.
    Forbidden(&'static str),
    /// 401: right place, wrong or missing token. Only this one is slowed down.
    Unauthorized,
}

pub struct RequestMeta<'a> {
    pub peer: IpAddr,
    pub host: Option<&'a str>,
    pub origin: Option<&'a str>,
    pub authorization: Option<&'a str>,
}

pub struct Guard {
    bind: SocketAddr,
    gateway: IpAddr,
    token: String,
}

/// An IPv4 peer can show up as an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address on a dual-stack socket.
fn canonical(ip: IpAddr) -> IpAddr {
    match ip {
        IpAddr::V6(v6) => v6.to_ipv4_mapped().map(IpAddr::V4).unwrap_or(ip),
        v4 => v4,
    }
}

impl Guard {
    pub fn new(bind: SocketAddr, gateway: IpAddr, token: String) -> Self {
        Guard { bind, gateway: canonical(gateway), token }
    }

    /// `Host` exactly as a client addressing the bound address would send it.
    pub fn expected_host(&self) -> String {
        match self.bind.ip() {
            IpAddr::V4(v4) => format!("{v4}:{}", self.bind.port()),
            IpAddr::V6(v6) => format!("[{v6}]:{}", self.bind.port()),
        }
    }

    pub fn check(&self, request: &RequestMeta) -> Result<(), Reject> {
        let peer = canonical(request.peer);
        if peer != self.gateway || !is_tailnet_ip(peer) {
            return Err(Reject::Forbidden("source"));
        }
        if request.host != Some(self.expected_host().as_str()) {
            return Err(Reject::Forbidden("host"));
        }
        if request.origin.is_some() {
            return Err(Reject::Forbidden("origin"));
        }
        let presented = request.authorization.and_then(|value| value.strip_prefix("Bearer ")).unwrap_or("");
        if !constant_time_eq(presented.as_bytes(), self.token.as_bytes()) {
            return Err(Reject::Unauthorized);
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

/// Length-independent comparison (a length difference still compares every byte of the
/// longer side, against a fixed filler).
pub fn constant_time_eq(a: &[u8], b: &[u8]) -> bool {
    let len = a.len().max(b.len());
    let pad = |s: &[u8]| {
        let mut v = vec![0xffu8; len];
        v[..s.len()].copy_from_slice(s);
        v
    };
    let same_length = (a.len() as u64).ct_eq(&(b.len() as u64));
    let same_bytes = pad(a).ct_eq(&pad(b));
    bool::from(same_length & same_bytes)
}

/// Slows down consecutive authentication failures: 100 ms, 200 ms, 400 ms ... up to 5 s.
/// A success resets it. Requests that authenticate are never delayed.
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct Backoff {
    failures: u32,
}
impl Backoff {
    pub const FIRST: Duration = Duration::from_millis(100);
    pub const MAX: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5);

    pub fn failed(&mut self) -> Duration {
        self.failures = self.failures.saturating_add(1);
        let shift = (self.failures - 1).min(10);
        (Self::FIRST * (1u32 << shift)).min(Self::MAX)
    }
    pub fn succeeded(&mut self) {
        self.failures = 0;
    }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/status.rs`:

```rust
//! The agent's status (spec D5): enumerations and a timestamp, nothing about screen content.
use std::fmt::Write as _;

/// P1 decides this. `true` until the lock-screen pre-test shows that capture and key
/// input both work on the login window; then the owner flips it (plan, Task 13).
pub const REFUSE_WHEN_LOCKED: bool = true;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Screen {
    Granted,
    Denied,
    /// Permission says yes but only content-free frames arrive (P4: the 30-day recheck).
    Suspect,
}
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Accessibility {
    Granted,
    Denied,
}
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum DisplayState {
    Ok,
    Asleep,
    None,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Status {
    pub screen: Screen,
    pub accessibility: Accessibility,
    pub display: DisplayState,
    pub locked: bool,
    /// Seconds since the epoch of the last captured frame that had content.
    pub last_capture_at: Option<u64>,
    pub version: &'static str,
}

/// Why a session is refused for a state reason; the gateway passes the code to the page.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Gate {
    Permission,
    Locked,
}
impl Gate {
    pub fn code(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Gate::Permission => "permission",
            Gate::Locked => "locked",
        }
    }
    pub fn http_status(self) -> u16 {
        match self {
            Gate::Permission => 403,
            Gate::Locked => 423,
        }
    }
}

impl Status {
    pub fn gate(&self, refuse_when_locked: bool) -> Result<(), Gate> {
        // `Suspect` still lets a session start: a session is how the suspicion is cleared.
        if self.screen == Screen::Denied || self.accessibility == Accessibility::Denied {
            return Err(Gate::Permission);
        }
        if self.locked && refuse_when_locked {
            return Err(Gate::Locked);
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn to_json(&self) -> String {
        let screen = match self.screen {
            Screen::Granted => "granted",
            Screen::Denied => "denied",
            Screen::Suspect => "suspect",
        };
        let accessibility = match self.accessibility {
            Accessibility::Granted => "granted",
            Accessibility::Denied => "denied",
        };
        let display = match self.display {
            DisplayState::Ok => "ok",
            DisplayState::Asleep => "asleep",
            DisplayState::None => "none",
        };
        let mut out = String::new();
        let _ = write!(
            out,
            "{{\"version\":\"{}\",\"screen\":\"{screen}\",\"accessibility\":\"{accessibility}\",\"display\":\"{display}\",\"locked\":{},\"lastCaptureAt\":",
            self.version, self.locked
        );
        match self.last_capture_at {
            Some(t) => {
                let _ = write!(out, "{t}");
            }
            None => out.push_str("null"),
        }
        out.push('}');
        out
    }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/size.rs`:

```rust
//! Stream size, frame rate and bitrate ceiling (spec D3).

pub const MIN_W: u32 = 640;
pub const MAX_W: u32 = 3840;
pub const MIN_H: u32 = 480;
pub const MAX_H: u32 = 2160;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Size {
    pub w: u32,
    pub h: u32,
}
impl Size {
    pub fn pixels(self) -> u64 {
        self.w as u64 * self.h as u64
    }
}

const QHD_PIXELS: u64 = 2560 * 1440;
const UHD_PIXELS: u64 = 3840 * 2160;

/// The page and the gateway already keep requests inside these limits and even; a value
/// from anywhere else is dropped rather than clamped. An odd value is rounded down.
pub fn parse_requested(w: i64, h: i64) -> Option<Size> {
    if !(MIN_W as i64..=MAX_W as i64).contains(&w) || !(MIN_H as i64..=MAX_H as i64).contains(&h) {
        return None;
    }
    Some(Size { w: (w as u32) & !1, h: (h as u32) & !1 })
}

/// The largest stream with the display's aspect ratio that fits in `requested` and is
/// never larger than the display's own pixels, with even sides.
pub fn fit_to_display(requested: Size, display_px: Size) -> Size {
    let scale = (requested.w as f64 / display_px.w as f64).min(requested.h as f64 / display_px.h as f64).min(1.0);
    let even = |v: f64| ((v.floor() as u32).max(2)) & !1;
    Size { w: even(display_px.w as f64 * scale), h: even(display_px.h as f64 * scale) }
}

/// 60 fps up to 2560x1440 pixels, 45 fps above (the M4 encoder tops out near 48 at 4K).
pub fn fps_for(size: Size) -> u32 {
    if size.pixels() <= QHD_PIXELS {
        60
    } else {
        45
    }
}

/// The ceiling the congestion controller works under: 12 Mbps up to 1440p, 20 Mbps at
/// 4K, linear in the pixel count between.
pub fn bitrate_cap(size: Size) -> u32 {
    let p = size.pixels();
    if p <= QHD_PIXELS {
        return 12_000_000;
    }
    if p >= UHD_PIXELS {
        return 20_000_000;
    }
    let t = (p - QHD_PIXELS) as f64 / (UHD_PIXELS - QHD_PIXELS) as f64;
    (12_000_000.0 + t * 8_000_000.0).round() as u32
}
```

`agent/mac/src/http.rs` (`route` holds every decision; `serve` only moves bytes; each request is handled on its own thread, at most 16 at once, so a slowed-down failed login never delays the gateway's own requests):

```rust
//! The agent's HTTP interface for the gateway (spec D2, D5, D7): `GET /v1/status` and
//! `POST /v1/sessions`. `route` holds every decision and is tested without a socket;
//! `serve` only moves bytes between tiny_http and `route`.
use crate::auth::{Backoff, Guard, Reject, RequestMeta};
use crate::size::{self, Size};
use crate::status::{Gate, Status};
use serde_json::{json, Value};
use std::io::Read;
use std::net::{IpAddr, SocketAddr};
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering::SeqCst};
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
use std::time::Duration;

pub const MAX_BODY: u64 = 64 * 1024;
/// Requests handled at once. Each waits on its own thread, so a slowed-down failed
/// login never delays a request that authenticates; beyond this the agent says 503.
pub const MAX_IN_FLIGHT: usize = 16;

pub struct SessionRequest {
    pub size: Size,
    pub sdp: String,
}

pub enum SessionFailure {
    Gate(Gate),
    /// The offer could not be used (400).
    BadOffer(String),
    /// Anything else (500). The text goes to the log, never to the caller.
    Internal(String),
}

pub trait Backend: Send + Sync {
    fn status(&self) -> Status;
    fn start_session(&self, request: SessionRequest) -> Result<String, SessionFailure>;
}

pub struct Head<'a> {
    pub method: &'a str,
    /// Path without a query string.
    pub path: &'a str,
    pub peer: IpAddr,
    pub host: Option<&'a str>,
    pub origin: Option<&'a str>,
    pub authorization: Option<&'a str>,
    pub content_type: Option<&'a str>,
    pub content_length: Option<u64>,
}

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct Reply {
    pub status: u16,
    pub body: String,
    /// How long to wait before answering (failed authentication only).
    pub delay: Duration,
}
impl Reply {
    fn json(status: u16, value: Value) -> Reply {
        Reply { status, body: value.to_string(), delay: Duration::ZERO }
    }
    fn error(status: u16, message: &str) -> Reply {
        Reply::json(status, json!({ "error": message }))
    }
}

pub struct Router {
    guard: Guard,
    backend: Arc<dyn Backend>,
    backoff: Mutex<Backoff>,
}

impl Router {
    pub fn new(guard: Guard, backend: Arc<dyn Backend>) -> Self {
        Router { guard, backend, backoff: Mutex::new(Backoff::default()) }
    }

    pub fn route(&self, head: &Head, body: &mut dyn Read) -> Reply {
        let meta =
            RequestMeta { peer: head.peer, host: head.host, origin: head.origin, authorization: head.authorization };
        match self.guard.check(&meta) {
            Ok(()) => self.backoff.lock().unwrap().succeeded(),
            Err(Reject::Forbidden(why)) => {
                eprintln!("[http] refused ({why})");
                return Reply::error(403, "forbidden");
            }
            Err(Reject::Unauthorized) => {
                eprintln!("[http] refused (token)");
                let delay = self.backoff.lock().unwrap().failed();
                return Reply { delay, ..Reply::error(401, "unauthorized") };
            }
        }
        match (head.method, head.path) {
            ("GET", "/v1/status") => {
                Reply { status: 200, body: self.backend.status().to_json(), delay: Duration::ZERO }
            }
            ("POST", "/v1/sessions") => self.create_session(head, body),
            (_, "/v1/status") | (_, "/v1/sessions") => Reply::error(405, "method not allowed"),
            _ => Reply::error(404, "not found"),
        }
    }

    fn create_session(&self, head: &Head, body: &mut dyn Read) -> Reply {
        if !head.content_type.map(|t| t.starts_with("application/json")).unwrap_or(false) {
            return Reply::error(415, "json required");
        }
        let Some(length) = head.content_length else {
            return Reply::error(411, "content-length required");
        };
        if length > MAX_BODY {
            return Reply::error(413, "body too large");
        }
        let mut bytes = Vec::with_capacity(length as usize);
        if body.take(length).read_to_end(&mut bytes).is_err() || bytes.len() as u64 != length {
            return Reply::error(400, "body incomplete");
        }
        let Ok(value) = serde_json::from_slice::<Value>(&bytes) else {
            return Reply::error(400, "invalid json");
        };
        let request = (|| {
            let width = value.get("width")?.as_i64()?;
            let height = value.get("height")?.as_i64()?;
            let sdp = value.get("sdp")?.as_str().filter(|s| s.starts_with("v=0"))?;
            Some(SessionRequest { size: size::parse_requested(width, height)?, sdp: sdp.to_string() })
        })();
        let Some(request) = request else {
            return Reply::error(400, "width, height and sdp are required");
        };
        match self.backend.start_session(request) {
            Ok(sdp) => Reply::json(200, json!({ "sdp": sdp })),
            Err(SessionFailure::Gate(gate)) => {
                Reply::json(gate.http_status(), json!({ "error": gate.code(), "code": gate.code() }))
            }
            Err(SessionFailure::BadOffer(why)) => {
                eprintln!("[http] bad offer: {why}");
                Reply::error(400, "offer rejected")
            }
            Err(SessionFailure::Internal(why)) => {
                eprintln!("[http] session failed: {why}");
                Reply::error(500, "session failed")
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Serves until the process ends. One thread per request, at most `MAX_IN_FLIGHT`.
pub fn serve(bind: SocketAddr, router: Arc<Router>) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let server = tiny_http::Server::http(bind).map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("cannot bind {bind}: {e}"))?;
    println!("[http] listening on {bind}");
    let in_flight = Arc::new(AtomicUsize::new(0));
    for request in server.incoming_requests() {
        if in_flight.fetch_add(1, SeqCst) >= MAX_IN_FLIGHT {
            in_flight.fetch_sub(1, SeqCst);
            let _ = request.respond(tiny_http::Response::from_string("busy").with_status_code(503));
            continue;
        }
        let (router, in_flight) = (router.clone(), in_flight.clone());
        std::thread::spawn(move || {
            handle(&router, request);
            in_flight.fetch_sub(1, SeqCst);
        });
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn header(request: &tiny_http::Request, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
    request
        .headers()
        .iter()
        .find(|h| h.field.as_str().as_str().eq_ignore_ascii_case(name))
        .map(|h| h.value.as_str().to_string())
}

fn handle(router: &Router, mut request: tiny_http::Request) {
    let Some(peer) = request.remote_addr().map(|a| a.ip()) else { return };
    let method = request.method().as_str().to_string();
    let path = request.url().split('?').next().unwrap_or("/").to_string();
    let (host, origin, authorization, content_type) = (
        header(&request, "Host"),
        header(&request, "Origin"),
        header(&request, "Authorization"),
        header(&request, "Content-Type"),
    );
    let head = Head {
        method: &method,
        path: &path,
        peer,
        host: host.as_deref(),
        origin: origin.as_deref(),
        authorization: authorization.as_deref(),
        content_type: content_type.as_deref(),
        content_length: request.body_length().map(|n| n as u64),
    };
    let reply = router.route(&head, request.as_reader());
    if !reply.delay.is_zero() {
        std::thread::sleep(reply.delay);
    }
    let header = tiny_http::Header::from_bytes(&b"Content-Type"[..], &b"application/json"[..]).unwrap();
    let _ = request
        .respond(tiny_http::Response::from_string(reply.body).with_status_code(reply.status).with_header(header));
}
```

`agent/mac/src/log_cap.rs` and `agent/mac/src/paths.rs`:

```rust
//! The log file is the agent's standard output, opened by launchd in append mode. launchd
//! cannot limit its size, so the agent does: when the file passes the cap it is emptied in
//! place (the open descriptor keeps appending at the new end). There are no per-frame log
//! lines, so the cap is only reached after weeks (spec D6).
use std::os::fd::RawFd;

pub const CAP_BYTES: u64 = 5 * 1024 * 1024;

pub fn should_truncate(size: u64) -> bool {
    size > CAP_BYTES
}

/// Empties the file behind `fd` if it is a regular file larger than the cap. Returns whether
/// it did. A pipe, a terminal or a file that cannot be examined is left alone.
pub fn truncate_if_large(fd: RawFd) -> bool {
    // SAFETY: fstat writes into the zeroed struct we own; ftruncate takes the descriptor by value.
    unsafe {
        let mut stat: libc::stat = std::mem::zeroed();
        if libc::fstat(fd, &mut stat) != 0 || (stat.st_mode & libc::S_IFMT) != libc::S_IFREG {
            return false;
        }
        should_truncate(stat.st_size as u64) && libc::ftruncate(fd, 0) == 0
    }
}
```

```rust
//! Where the settings and the token live. Fixed at build time: the agent takes no
//! command-line argument and reads no environment variable (spec D2), so nobody who can
//! only start the program can point it at a settings file of their own.
//!
//! The operational build reads the root-owned `/usr/local/etc/gome-remote-agent/agent.json`.
//! A `dev-install` build (spec O4) reads a user-owned directory instead, so the binary
//! can be replaced during development without administrator rights.
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ConfigOwner {
    Root,
    CurrentUser,
}

#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Paths {
    pub config: PathBuf,
    pub token: PathBuf,
    pub config_owner: ConfigOwner,
}

#[cfg(not(feature = "dev-install"))]
pub fn locate(home: &Path) -> Paths {
    Paths {
        config: PathBuf::from("/usr/local/etc/gome-remote-agent/agent.json"),
        token: home.join("Library/Application Support/Gome Remote Agent/token"),
        config_owner: ConfigOwner::Root,
    }
}

#[cfg(feature = "dev-install")]
pub fn locate(home: &Path) -> Paths {
    let dir = home.join("Library/Application Support/Gome Remote Agent Dev");
    Paths { config: dir.join("agent.json"), token: dir.join("token"), config_owner: ConfigOwner::CurrentUser }
}

/// The home directory from the password database, not from `$HOME`.
pub fn home_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
    use std::ffi::CStr;
    use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt;
    // SAFETY: getpwuid_r writes into the buffer we own and returns pointers into it;
    // the directory string is copied out before the buffer goes away.
    unsafe {
        let mut passwd: libc::passwd = std::mem::zeroed();
        let mut buffer = vec![0 as libc::c_char; 4096];
        let mut result: *mut libc::passwd = std::ptr::null_mut();
        let rc = libc::getpwuid_r(libc::getuid(), &mut passwd, buffer.as_mut_ptr(), buffer.len(), &mut result);
        if rc != 0 || result.is_null() || passwd.pw_dir.is_null() {
            return None;
        }
        let dir = CStr::from_ptr(passwd.pw_dir);
        Some(PathBuf::from(std::ffi::OsStr::from_bytes(dir.to_bytes())))
    }
}
```

```rust
// ---- additions for the production agent (not in the spike) ----

pub const kCFStringEncodingUTF8: u32 = 0x0800_0100;

#[link(name = "CoreFoundation", kind = "framework")]
extern "C" {
    pub fn CFStringCreateWithCString(alloc: CFTypeRef, c_str: *const std::os::raw::c_char, encoding: u32) -> CFTypeRef;
}

/// CGEvent type codes used by the injector.
pub const kCGEventLeftMouseDown: u32 = 1;
pub const kCGEventLeftMouseUp: u32 = 2;
pub const kCGEventRightMouseDown: u32 = 3;
pub const kCGEventRightMouseUp: u32 = 4;
pub const kCGEventMouseMoved: u32 = 5;
pub const kCGEventLeftMouseDragged: u32 = 6;
pub const kCGEventRightMouseDragged: u32 = 7;
pub const kCGEventKeyDown: u32 = 10;
pub const kCGEventKeyUp: u32 = 11;
pub const kCGEventFlagsChanged: u32 = 12;
pub const kCGEventOtherMouseDown: u32 = 25;
pub const kCGEventOtherMouseUp: u32 = 26;
pub const kCGEventOtherMouseDragged: u32 = 27;
/// CGEventField values.
pub const kCGMouseEventClickState: u32 = 1;
pub const kCGKeyboardEventAutorepeat: u32 = 8;

#[link(name = "CoreGraphics", kind = "framework")]
extern "C" {
    pub fn CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary() -> CFTypeRef;
    pub fn CGDisplayIsAsleep(d: u32) -> u32;
    pub fn CGDisplayCopyDisplayMode(d: u32) -> CFTypeRef;
    pub fn CGDisplayModeGetPixelWidth(mode: CFTypeRef) -> usize;
    pub fn CGDisplayModeGetPixelHeight(mode: CFTypeRef) -> usize;
    pub fn CGEventCreateKeyboardEvent(src: CFTypeRef, key: u16, down: bool) -> CFTypeRef;
    pub fn CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString(ev: CFTypeRef, len: usize, chars: *const u16);
    pub fn CGEventSetFlags(ev: CFTypeRef, flags: u64);
    pub fn CGEventSetType(ev: CFTypeRef, ty: u32);
    pub fn CGEventSetIntegerValueField(ev: CFTypeRef, field: u32, value: i64);
    pub fn CGEventCreateScrollWheelEvent2(
        src: CFTypeRef,
        units: u32,
        wheel_count: u32,
        wheel1: i32,
        wheel2: i32,
        wheel3: i32,
    ) -> CFTypeRef;
}

#[link(name = "IOKit", kind = "framework")]
extern "C" {
    pub fn IOPMAssertionDeclareUserActivity(name: CFTypeRef, kind: u32, id: *mut u32) -> i32;
    pub fn IOPMAssertionCreateWithName(kind: CFTypeRef, level: u32, name: CFTypeRef, id: *mut u32) -> i32;
    pub fn IOPMAssertionRelease(id: u32) -> i32;
}

/// A CFString from a Rust string (the caller owns it through `Cf`).
pub fn cf_string(text: &str) -> Cf {
    let c = std::ffi::CString::new(text).expect("no NUL in a constant string");
    Cf(unsafe { CFStringCreateWithCString(std::ptr::null(), c.as_ptr(), kCFStringEncodingUTF8) })
}
```

`agent/mac/src/mac/system.rs` (permissions, display state, lock screen, native pixels, main display bounds):

```rust
//! What the status reports and what a session start needs to know about the system:
//! permissions, the display, the lock screen, the screen size.
use super::ffi::*;
use crate::size::Size;
use crate::status::DisplayState;

pub fn screen_recording_granted() -> bool {
    unsafe { CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess() }
}

pub fn accessibility_granted() -> bool {
    unsafe { AXIsProcessTrusted() }
}

/// Asks macOS for both permissions. This is what makes the binary appear in System
/// Settings so the owner can switch it on; it does nothing for a permission already given.
pub fn prompt_for_permissions() {
    unsafe {
        if !CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess() {
            let _ = CGRequestScreenCaptureAccess();
        }
        let options = cf_dict(&[(kAXTrustedCheckOptionPrompt, kCFBooleanTrue)]);
        let _ = AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions(options.0);
    }
}

pub fn display_state() -> DisplayState {
    unsafe {
        let id = CGMainDisplayID();
        if id == 0 {
            DisplayState::None
        } else if CGDisplayIsAsleep(id) != 0 {
            DisplayState::Asleep
        } else {
            DisplayState::Ok
        }
    }
}

/// True while the login window or lock screen is up (`CGSSessionScreenIsLocked`).
pub fn screen_locked() -> bool {
    unsafe {
        let session = CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary();
        if session.is_null() {
            return false;
        }
        let key = cf_string("CGSSessionScreenIsLocked");
        let value = CFDictionaryGetValue(session, key.0);
        let locked = !value.is_null() && value == kCFBooleanTrue;
        CFRelease(session);
        locked
    }
}

/// The main display's size in real pixels (a 4K panel shown as 1920x1080 points is 3840x2160).
pub fn native_pixels() -> Option<Size> {
    unsafe {
        let mode = CGDisplayCopyDisplayMode(CGMainDisplayID());
        if mode.is_null() {
            return None;
        }
        let size = Size { w: CGDisplayModeGetPixelWidth(mode) as u32, h: CGDisplayModeGetPixelHeight(mode) as u32 };
        CFRelease(mode);
        (size.w > 0 && size.h > 0).then_some(size)
    }
}

/// The main display's bounds in points (the coordinate space of mouse events).
pub fn main_bounds() -> CGRect {
    unsafe { CGDisplayBounds(CGMainDisplayID()) }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/mac/mod.rs` (as of this task; later tasks extend it). It checks the settings and token the way D6 says (it refuses to start otherwise), registers the program in System Settings when a permission is missing, empties the log at 5 MiB, and serves HTTP with a backend that answers `/v1/status` truthfully and cannot start sessions yet:

```rust
//! macOS side: framework bindings and the process entry point.
pub mod ffi;
pub mod system;

use crate::auth::Guard;
use crate::config::{self, AgentConfig, FileMeta, Kind};
use crate::http::{self, Backend, Router, SessionFailure, SessionRequest};
use crate::paths::{self, ConfigOwner, Paths};
use crate::status::{Accessibility, Screen, Status};
use std::net::SocketAddr;
use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;
use std::path::Path;
use std::sync::Arc;

fn kind_of(meta: &std::fs::Metadata) -> Kind {
    let kind = meta.file_type();
    if kind.is_file() {
        Kind::File
    } else if kind.is_dir() {
        Kind::Dir
    } else {
        Kind::Other
    }
}

fn meta_of(path: &Path) -> Result<FileMeta, String> {
    // symlink_metadata: a symbolic link is never followed, so it is not a regular file.
    let meta = std::fs::symlink_metadata(path).map_err(|e| format!("{}: {e}", path.display()))?;
    Ok(FileMeta { mode: meta.mode() & 0o7777, uid: meta.uid(), kind: kind_of(&meta) })
}

fn load_config(paths: &Paths, user_uid: u32) -> Result<AgentConfig, String> {
    let owner = match paths.config_owner {
        ConfigOwner::Root => 0,
        ConfigOwner::CurrentUser => user_uid,
    };
    let file = meta_of(&paths.config)?;
    config::check_config_file(&file, owner, user_uid).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let dir = paths.config.parent().ok_or("the settings file has no directory")?;
    config::check_config_dir(&meta_of(dir)?, owner).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&paths.config).map_err(|e| format!("{}: {e}", paths.config.display()))?;
    config::parse_config(&text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())
}

fn load_token(paths: &Paths, user_uid: u32) -> Result<String, String> {
    config::check_token_file(&meta_of(&paths.token)?, user_uid).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&paths.token).map_err(|e| format!("{}: {e}", paths.token.display()))?;
    config::parse_token(&text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())
}

/// The agent as of this task: it answers `/v1/status` truthfully and refuses to start
/// sessions (capture, WebRTC and input arrive in the next tasks).
struct StatusOnly;

impl Backend for StatusOnly {
    fn status(&self) -> Status {
        Status {
            screen: if system::screen_recording_granted() { Screen::Granted } else { Screen::Denied },
            accessibility: if system::accessibility_granted() { Accessibility::Granted } else { Accessibility::Denied },
            display: system::display_state(),
            locked: system::screen_locked(),
            last_capture_at: None,
            version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
        }
    }

    fn start_session(&self, _request: SessionRequest) -> Result<String, SessionFailure> {
        Err(SessionFailure::Internal("this build cannot start sessions yet".into()))
    }
}

pub fn run() {
    let refuse = |message: String| -> ! {
        eprintln!("gome-remote-agent refused to start: {message}");
        std::process::exit(2)
    };
    let home = paths::home_dir().unwrap_or_else(|| refuse("no home directory".into()));
    let paths = paths::locate(&home);
    let user_uid = unsafe { libc::getuid() };
    let config = load_config(&paths, user_uid).unwrap_or_else(|e| refuse(e));
    let token = load_token(&paths, user_uid).unwrap_or_else(|e| refuse(e));

    println!("[perm] screen={} accessibility={}", system::screen_recording_granted(), system::accessibility_granted());
    if !system::screen_recording_granted() || !system::accessibility_granted() {
        // Registers the binary in System Settings; the status keeps saying "permission" until allowed.
        system::prompt_for_permissions();
    }

    // launchd cannot cap the log it writes for us; the agent empties it when it grows too big.
    std::thread::spawn(|| loop {
        crate::log_cap::truncate_if_large(1);
        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(60));
    });

    let address = SocketAddr::new(config.bind, config.port);
    let router = Arc::new(Router::new(Guard::new(address, config.gateway, token), Arc::new(StatusOnly)));
    std::thread::spawn(move || {
        if let Err(e) = http::serve(address, router) {
            eprintln!("gome-remote-agent: {e:#}");
            std::process::exit(4);
        }
    });
    // ScreenCaptureKit and the event posting want a live main run loop.
    unsafe { ffi::CFRunLoopRun() };
}
```

Create a development settings directory and token, and start the debug build, in a shell **on the Mac** (`ssh "$MAC"`; Task 11's `install.sh --dev` automates this later; the addresses are yours and are typed into the shell only, never into a file of the repository):

```sh
MAC_IP=<the Mac's Tailscale IPv4>; GATEWAY_IP=<the gateway server's Tailscale IPv4>
D="$HOME/Library/Application Support/Gome Remote Agent Dev"; mkdir -p "$D"; chmod 700 "$D"
printf '{"bind":"%s","port":18472,"gateway":"%s"}\n' "$MAC_IP" "$GATEWAY_IP" > "$D/agent.json"; chmod 644 "$D/agent.json"
(umask 077; openssl rand -base64 32 | tr '+/' '-_' | tr -d '=\n' > "$D/token")

sleep 2; cat /tmp/gome-agent-dev.log          # "[perm] screen=... accessibility=..." and "[http] listening on <MAC_IP>:18472"
```

Back on the gateway server, fetch the token for the commands below (`TOKEN=$(ssh "$MAC" 'cat "$HOME/Library/Application Support/Gome Remote Agent Dev/token"')`; `MAC_IP` is the same address as above).

Then from the **gateway server**:

```sh
curl -s -i -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "http://$MAC_IP:18472/v1/status"          # 200, JSON with six fields
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Authorization: Bearer wrong" "http://$MAC_IP:18472/v1/status"      # 401 (after a short delay)
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Origin: http://example.com" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "http://$MAC_IP:18472/v1/status"   # 403
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -H "Host: example.com" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" "http://$MAC_IP:18472/v1/status"            # 403
curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"width":1920,"height":1080,"sdp":"v=0"}' "http://$MAC_IP:18472/v1/sessions"   # 500: sessions are not built yet
```

Expected: the status body looks like `{"version":"0.1.0","screen":"denied","accessibility":"denied","display":"ok","locked":false,"lastCaptureAt":null}` (permissions read `denied` until the owner allows the binary; that is expected from an ssh-started process); the three refusals print 401, 403, 403; the last prints 500. From the **Mac itself** (`curl http://$MAC_IP:18472/v1/status`) the answer is 403: the agent only talks to the gateway. Stop the agent afterwards with the PID file written above (`ssh "$MAC" 'kill $(cat /tmp/gome-agent-dev.pid)'`; never kill by process name) and remove the development directory if you will not reuse it.

### Task 7: Capture and encode (ScreenCaptureKit, VideoToolbox, bitrate, keepalive, black frames, display wake)

Spec: D3 (capture without cursor, queue depth 3, NV12, wake and keep the display awake, sizes and frame rates, H.264 settings, bitrate from the estimate with a 2 Mbps floor, static-screen repeats), D5 (`lastCaptureAt` counts frames with content; P4 black-frame suspicion), S4 (bitrate controller).

- Create: `agent/mac/src/{bitrate,keepalive,black,frame}.rs`, `agent/mac/src/mac/{wake,capture,encoder,pipeline}.rs`, `agent/mac/examples/capture_smoke.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes (Task 6): `size::{Size, fps_for, bitrate_cap}`, `status::DisplayState`, `mac::ffi`, `mac::system::display_state`.

`agent/mac/src/bitrate.rs`: the session starts below the ceiling; a low estimate cuts to the 2 Mbps floor and no lower; the rate follows the estimate with 15 % headroom up to the ceiling; changes under 10 % do not reconfigure the encoder; a smaller stream lowers the ceiling and the current rate.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn it_starts_below_the_ceiling_and_never_above_it() {
        assert_eq!(BitrateController::new(12_000_000).current(), START_BPS);
        assert_eq!(BitrateController::new(4_000_000).current(), 4_000_000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_low_estimate_cuts_the_bitrate_to_the_floor_and_no_lower() {
        let mut c = BitrateController::new(12_000_000);
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(500_000), Some(FLOOR_BPS));
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(100_000), None, "already at the floor");
        assert_eq!(c.current(), FLOOR_BPS);
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_bitrate_follows_the_estimate_with_headroom_and_stops_at_the_ceiling() {
        let mut c = BitrateController::new(12_000_000);
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(10_000_000), Some(8_500_000));
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(80_000_000), Some(12_000_000));
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(90_000_000), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn small_changes_do_not_reconfigure_the_encoder() {
        let mut c = BitrateController::new(12_000_000);
        c.on_estimate(10_000_000);
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(10_400_000), None);
        assert_eq!(c.on_estimate(9_700_000), None);
        assert_eq!(c.current(), 8_500_000);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_smaller_stream_lowers_the_ceiling_and_the_current_rate() {
        let mut c = BitrateController::new(20_000_000);
        c.on_estimate(60_000_000);
        assert_eq!(c.current(), 20_000_000);
        assert_eq!(c.set_cap(12_000_000), Some(12_000_000));
        assert_eq!(c.set_cap(20_000_000), None, "raising the ceiling waits for an estimate");
        assert_eq!(c.current(), 12_000_000);
    }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/keepalive.rs`: a busy screen never repeats; a still screen repeats after 100 ms and no faster; a pending keyframe waits at most 40 ms.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn ages(capture_ms: u64, submit_ms: u64, key: bool) -> Ages {
        Ages {
            since_capture: Duration::from_millis(capture_ms),
            since_submit: Duration::from_millis(submit_ms),
            keyframe_pending: key,
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_busy_screen_never_repeats() {
        assert!(!should_resubmit(&ages(8, 8, false)));
        assert!(!should_resubmit(&ages(100, 3, false)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_still_screen_repeats_after_100_ms_and_no_faster() {
        assert!(!should_resubmit(&ages(100, 100, false)));
        assert!(should_resubmit(&ages(101, 101, false)));
        // Just repeated: wait another interval even though the capture is old.
        assert!(!should_resubmit(&ages(900, 5, false)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_pending_keyframe_does_not_wait_for_the_interval() {
        assert!(!should_resubmit(&ages(30, 30, true)));
        assert!(should_resubmit(&ages(41, 41, true)));
        assert!(!should_resubmit(&ages(41, 41, false)));
    }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/black.rs`: the luma sampling (including a row stride wider than the width and planes too small to read) and the five-second rule.

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn a_uniformly_dark_plane_is_black_and_any_bright_sample_is_not() {
        let (w, h) = (64, 36);
        let mut plane = vec![16u8; w * h];
        assert!(luma_is_black(&plane, w, h, w));
        plane[(h - 1) * w + (w - 1)] = 200; // the last grid sample is the bottom-right pixel
        assert!(!luma_is_black(&plane, w, h, w));
        plane[(h - 1) * w + (w - 1)] = 20;
        assert!(luma_is_black(&plane, w, h, w), "20 is still within the noise allowance");
        plane[0] = 21;
        assert!(!luma_is_black(&plane, w, h, w));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_row_stride_wider_than_the_width_is_respected() {
        let (w, h, stride) = (64, 36, 80);
        let mut plane = vec![255u8; stride * h]; // padding bytes are bright and must be ignored
        for y in 0..h {
            for x in 0..w {
                plane[y * stride + x] = 16;
            }
        }
        assert!(luma_is_black(&plane, w, h, stride));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_plane_that_is_too_small_does_not_panic() {
        assert!(luma_is_black(&[], 64, 36, 64));
        assert!(luma_is_black(&[0u8; 10], 64, 36, 64));
        assert!(luma_is_black(&[0u8; 64 * 36], 0, 36, 64));
        assert!(luma_is_black(&[0u8; 64 * 36], 64, 36, 32), "stride smaller than width");
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_five_seconds_of_unbroken_black_is_suspect() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut d = BlackDetector::default();
        d.observe(t0, false);
        d.observe(t0 + Duration::from_secs(1), true);
        assert!(!d.suspect(t0 + Duration::from_secs(5)));
        assert!(d.suspect(t0 + Duration::from_secs(6)));
        d.observe(t0 + Duration::from_secs(7), true);
        assert!(d.suspect(t0 + Duration::from_secs(7)), "black_since keeps the first black frame");
        d.observe(t0 + Duration::from_secs(8), false);
        assert!(!d.suspect(t0 + Duration::from_secs(60)));
        assert_eq!(d.last_content(), Some(t0 + Duration::from_secs(8)));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reset_forgets_everything() {
        let t0 = Instant::now();
        let mut d = BlackDetector::default();
        d.observe(t0, true);
        d.reset();
        assert!(!d.suspect(t0 + Duration::from_secs(60)));
        assert_eq!(d.last_content(), None);
    }
}
```

```rust
//! What the encoder hands to the peer.
use std::time::Instant;

/// Where encoded frames go: the session's WebRTC peer (peer.rs), or a stand-in in tests.
pub trait FrameTarget: Send + Sync {
    /// False when the target is gone and wants no more frames.
    fn send_frame(&self, frame: EncodedFrame) -> bool;
}

pub struct EncodedFrame {
    /// Annex B access unit (SPS and PPS in front of every keyframe).
    pub data: Vec<u8>,
    pub key: bool,
    /// When the frame was handed to the encoder (that is, when it was captured).
    pub submitted: Instant,
}
```

`agent/mac/src/bitrate.rs` (follow the TWCC estimate down at once, up with headroom, never below 2 Mbps or above the pixel-count ceiling):

```rust
//! Encoder bitrate from the sender-side bandwidth estimate (str0m's TWCC, spec D3):
//! follow the estimate down at once, climb back more carefully, never below the floor or
//! above the pixel-count ceiling.

pub const FLOOR_BPS: u32 = 2_000_000;
/// What a session starts at before the first estimate: below any ceiling, so a slow path
/// is not flooded while the estimator is still learning.
pub const START_BPS: u32 = 6_000_000;
/// Headroom left under the estimate for retransmissions and keyframes.
const USE_FRACTION: f64 = 0.85;
/// A change smaller than this is not worth reconfiguring the encoder for.
const MIN_STEP: f64 = 0.10;

pub struct BitrateController {
    cap: u32,
    current: u32,
}

impl BitrateController {
    pub fn new(cap: u32) -> Self {
        BitrateController { cap: cap.max(FLOOR_BPS), current: START_BPS.clamp(FLOOR_BPS, cap.max(FLOOR_BPS)) }
    }
    pub fn current(&self) -> u32 {
        self.current
    }
    pub fn cap(&self) -> u32 {
        self.cap
    }

    /// A new estimate arrived. Returns the bitrate to set when it changes meaningfully.
    pub fn on_estimate(&mut self, estimate_bps: u64) -> Option<u32> {
        let wanted = ((estimate_bps as f64 * USE_FRACTION) as u64).clamp(FLOOR_BPS as u64, self.cap as u64) as u32;
        let step = (wanted as f64 - self.current as f64).abs() / self.current as f64;
        // A change under 10 % is not worth reconfiguring the encoder for, unless it reaches the
        // floor or the ceiling.
        if wanted == self.current || step < MIN_STEP && wanted != FLOOR_BPS && wanted != self.cap {
            return None;
        }
        self.current = wanted;
        Some(wanted)
    }

    /// The stream size changed, and with it the ceiling.
    pub fn set_cap(&mut self, cap: u32) -> Option<u32> {
        self.cap = cap.max(FLOOR_BPS);
        if self.current > self.cap {
            self.current = self.cap;
            return Some(self.current);
        }
        None
    }
}
```

`agent/mac/src/keepalive.rs` (ScreenCaptureKit delivers frames only when the screen changes; the spike found that a 1 s repeat leaves 10 to 15 % of the first frames after a still period waiting 250 to 700 ms in Chrome's software decoder, and a 100 ms repeat hides it; P2 may change the constant, nothing else):

```rust
//! When the capture side re-submits the last frame (spec D3, "정지 화면"). ScreenCaptureKit
//! only delivers a frame when the screen changes, so a static screen would send nothing.
//! The spike found that with a 1 s repeat 10-15 % of the first frames after a still period
//! sat 250-700 ms in Chrome's software decoder; a 100 ms repeat (about 10 tiny frames a
//! second, 0.35-0.9 Mbps) hides that. P2 may change the constant, nothing else.
use std::time::Duration;

pub const KEEPALIVE: Duration = Duration::from_millis(100);
/// A requested keyframe (new viewer, PLI/FIR) waits at most this long for a real frame
/// before the last one is re-encoded as a keyframe.
pub const KEYFRAME_WAIT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(40);

pub struct Ages {
    /// Since the last frame ScreenCaptureKit delivered.
    pub since_capture: Duration,
    /// Since the last time anything (captured or repeated) went to the encoder.
    pub since_submit: Duration,
    pub keyframe_pending: bool,
}

pub fn should_resubmit(ages: &Ages) -> bool {
    let age = ages.since_capture.min(ages.since_submit);
    (ages.keyframe_pending && age > KEYFRAME_WAIT) || age > KEEPALIVE
}
```

`agent/mac/src/black.rs`:

```rust
//! Detects "permission lapsed, but ScreenCaptureKit keeps delivering empty frames" (P4):
//! the status then reads `suspect` and `lastCaptureAt` stops moving.
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

/// Only content-free frames for this long make the screen suspect.
pub const SUSPECT_AFTER: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5);
/// 16 is video-range black in NV12 luma; allow a little noise above it.
const BLACK_MAX: u8 = 20;

/// Samples a 32 x 18 grid of an NV12 luma plane (`stride` bytes per row). A plane that is
/// too small for its stated size counts as black rather than panicking.
pub fn luma_is_black(luma: &[u8], width: usize, height: usize, stride: usize) -> bool {
    if width == 0 || height == 0 || stride < width || luma.len() < stride * (height - 1) + width {
        return true;
    }
    for gy in 0..18 {
        let y = (gy * (height - 1)) / 17;
        for gx in 0..32 {
            let x = (gx * (width - 1)) / 31;
            if luma[y * stride + x] > BLACK_MAX {
                return false;
            }
        }
    }
    true
}

#[derive(Default)]
pub struct BlackDetector {
    black_since: Option<Instant>,
    last_content: Option<Instant>,
}

impl BlackDetector {
    /// Call for every frame ScreenCaptureKit delivers.
    pub fn observe(&mut self, now: Instant, black: bool) {
        if black {
            self.black_since.get_or_insert(now);
        } else {
            self.black_since = None;
            self.last_content = Some(now);
        }
    }
    /// A new session starts from a clean slate.
    pub fn reset(&mut self) {
        *self = BlackDetector::default();
    }
    pub fn suspect(&self, now: Instant) -> bool {
        self.black_since.map(|since| now.saturating_duration_since(since) >= SUSPECT_AFTER).unwrap_or(false)
    }
    /// The last time a frame with content arrived (the status' `lastCaptureAt`).
    pub fn last_content(&self) -> Option<Instant> {
        self.last_content
    }
}
```
